```json
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/method.tex",
    "oldString": "OBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).",
    "newString": "OBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\nEnd-to-end inference (scorer log-prob + feature extraction + LightGBM) runs at ${\\sim}$22 docs/sec on a single GPU without batching (Appendix~\\ref{sec:computation_cost}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Corpus-level statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction. (Simplified notation; see Eq.~\\ref{eq:kstar} for the formal $\\Kempirical$ definition.)}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: mutual information with the seed text degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this motivates the existence of a finite discrimination boundary where $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ falls below a decision threshold, though AUC and mutual information are not formally equivalent; the actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.\nPairwise AUC may exhibit non-monotonicity at deeper depths due to classifier and feature interactions (e.g., Pythia-1.4B greedy 4v5 $\\auc = 1.0$ vs.\\ 3v4 $= 0.522$; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}), reinforcing that $\\Kempirical$ is a measured quantity rather than a theoretical prediction.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\!\\bigl\\{k \\!\\geq\\! 1 : \\auc_{j-1,j} > \\theta,\\;\\forall\\, 1 \\!\\leq\\! j \\!\\leq\\! k\\bigr\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich requires all pairs up to depth $k$ to exceed the threshold (the $\\forall j$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ decreases monotonically above $\\theta$ across all tested configurations; Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).\nThe theoretical upper bound $\\Ktheory$ (the true maximum discriminable depth given optimal features) is not directly estimated in this work.\nSince any specific classifier on a feature subset achieves AUC at most equal to the Bayes-optimal classifier on all features, $\\Kempirical \\leq \\Ktheory$ follows directly.\nThe DPI motivates the empirical observation that $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ generally decreases with $k$ under non-degenerate decoding, though AUC is not a monotone function of MI (as noted above).\nHere $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point; $\\theta$ sits between the 1v2 AUC cluster ($0.61$--$0.70$) and the 2v3 cluster ($0.55$--$0.59$), so that $\\Kempirical$ is insensitive to the exact threshold within this range (justified below; Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).\nAll pairwise significance tests use 10{,}000-iteration permutation tests with Bonferroni correction for 10 comparisons ($\\alpha_{\\text{corrected}}{=}0.005$); we report bootstrap 95\\% CIs (1{,}000 resamples unless otherwise noted) for all boundary-pair AUCs and explicitly mark marginal cases where Bonferroni-corrected lower bounds fall below $\\theta$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold justification.}\n$\\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by two independent lines of evidence.\nFirst, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).\nSecond, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity that ensures any $\\theta \\in [0.59, 0.61]$ yields the same $\\Kempirical$; only at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\\theta \\in [0.55, 0.70]$.\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).\nSelf-BLEU reference sets are drawn exclusively within each fold to prevent information leakage across cross-validation splits; self-BLEU ranks \\#1 in 1/8 conditions (greedy decoding) but is absent from the top-3 in all 7 others.\nRemoving self-BLEU reduces mean $\\auc_{1v2}$ by $< 1$\\,pp across all models (14D $\\Kempirical = 2$ for all 4 configurations); in deployment where reference corpora are unavailable, self-BLEU can be replaced with corpus-level self-similarity or omitted entirely, leaving 14 features (surprisal statistics, TTR, hapax ratio, frequency tail) that require no external reference and retain full $\\Kempirical$.\nThese features are generator-agnostic in that they require no knowledge of or access to the generating model, though they depend on an external scorer for surprisal computation (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).\nOBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate six primary configurations spanning three model families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base), Qwen2.5-7B, Pythia-1.4B, and OLMo-1B; four additional models are introduced in Section~\\ref{sec:experiments}.\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \\textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \\textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \\textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",
```

```json
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/method.tex",
    "oldString": "OBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\nEnd-to-end inference (scorer log-prob + feature extraction + LightGBM) runs at ${\\sim}$22 docs/sec on a single GPU without batching (Appendix~\\ref{sec:computation_cost}).",
    "newString": "OBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Corpus-level statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction. (Simplified notation; see Eq.~\\ref{eq:kstar} for the formal $\\Kempirical$ definition.)}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: mutual information with the seed text degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this motivates the existence of a finite discrimination boundary where $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ falls below a decision threshold, though AUC and mutual information are not formally equivalent; the actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.\nPairwise AUC may exhibit non-monotonicity at deeper depths due to classifier and feature interactions (e.g., Pythia-1.4B greedy 4v5 $\\auc = 1.0$ vs.\\ 3v4 $= 0.522$; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}), reinforcing that $\\Kempirical$ is a measured quantity rather than a theoretical prediction.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\!\\bigl\\{k \\!\\geq\\! 1 : \\auc_{j-1,j} > \\theta,\\;\\forall\\, 1 \\!\\leq\\! j \\!\\leq\\! k\\bigr\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich requires all pairs up to depth $k$ to exceed the threshold (the $\\forall j$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ decreases monotonically above $\\theta$ across all tested configurations; Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).\nThe theoretical upper bound $\\Ktheory$ (the true maximum discriminable depth given optimal features) is not directly estimated in this work.\nSince any specific classifier on a feature subset achieves AUC at most equal to the Bayes-optimal classifier on all features, $\\Kempirical \\leq \\Ktheory$ follows directly.\nThe DPI motivates the empirical observation that $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ generally decreases with $k$ under non-degenerate decoding, though AUC is not a monotone function of MI (as noted above).\nHere $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point; $\\theta$ sits between the 1v2 AUC cluster ($0.61$--$0.70$) and the 2v3 cluster ($0.55$--$0.59$), so that $\\Kempirical$ is insensitive to the exact threshold within this range (justified below; Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).\nAll pairwise significance tests use 10{,}000-iteration permutation tests with Bonferroni correction for 10 comparisons ($\\alpha_{\\text{corrected}}{=}0.005$); we report bootstrap 95\\% CIs (1{,}000 resamples unless otherwise noted) for all boundary-pair AUCs and explicitly mark marginal cases where Bonferroni-corrected lower bounds fall below $\\theta$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold justification.}\n$\\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by two independent lines of evidence.\nFirst, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).\nSecond, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity that ensures any $\\theta \\in [0.59, 0.61]$ yields the same $\\Kempirical$; only at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\\theta \\in [0.55, 0.70]$.\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).\nSelf-BLEU reference sets are drawn exclusively within each fold to prevent information leakage across cross-validation splits; self-BLEU ranks \\#1 in 1/8 conditions (greedy decoding) but is absent from the top-3 in all 7 others.\nRemoving self-BLEU reduces mean $\\auc_{1v2}$ by $< 1$\\,pp across all models (14D $\\Kempirical = 2$ for all 4 configurations); in deployment where reference corpora are unavailable, self-BLEU can be replaced with corpus-level self-similarity or omitted entirely, leaving 14 features (surprisal statistics, TTR, hapax ratio, frequency tail) that require no external reference and retain full $\\Kempirical$.\nThese features are generator-agnostic in that they require no knowledge of or access to the generating model, though they depend on an external scorer for surprisal computation (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).\nOBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\nEnd-to-end inference (scorer log-prob + feature extraction + LightGBM) runs at ${\\sim}$22 docs/sec on a single GPU without batching (Appendix~\\ref{sec:computation_cost}).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate six primary configurations spanning three model families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base), Qwen2.5-7B, Pythia-1.4B, and OLMo-1B; four additional models are introduced in Section~\\ref{sec:experiments}.\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \\textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \\textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \\textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nOne-wash laundering refers to this concentrated signal loss at the 0v1 boundary: while depth-1 remains marginally distinguishable from depth-2 (1v2 $\\auc$ 0.61--0.70, the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ finding), the dominant distributional shift occurs at generation $0{\\to}1$, and beyond depth~2 no further signal is exploitable.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nOne-wash laundering is distinct from model collapse~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse}: model collapse describes cumulative quality degradation across generations, while one-wash laundering describes the erasure of statistical fingerprints distinguishing adjacent generations; text quality may continue degrading even after the features separating generation $k$ from $k{+}1$ become indistinguishable.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nThe 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.\nThe gap between $\\Kempirical$ and $\\Ktheory$ remains open; a DeBERTa-v3-base upper-bound probe yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = \\Kempirical^{\\text{OBD}} = 2$ on C4 and Code but $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = 4$ on arXiv, placing the cost of generator-agnostic features at one depth level for structured text and zero for web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd}).\nFeature rankings are scorer-independent ($\\rho > 0.90$); $\\Kempirical$ agreement is scorer-independent for strong-signal configurations (e.g., Qwen-rewrite retains $\\Kempirical{=}2$ under all scorers) but scorer-sensitive at marginal boundaries (Qwen-continuation drops to $\\Kempirical{=}1$; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nSelf-scoring inflates pairwise AUC: OLMo shows the largest gap (1v2 $\\Delta$ = +11.5\\,pp; self-scored $\\Kempirical$=2 vs.\\ cross-scored $\\Kempirical$=1), followed by Pythia (+4.7\\,pp) and Mistral-7B (+2.4\\,pp).\nCross-scoring provides a conservative, deployment-realistic estimate of $K^*$, as real-world contamination originates from unknown generators.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) and Code (Python) consistently yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across three independent architecture families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), as formulaic structure (citations, math notation, section conventions) creates larger per-generation distributional shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4).\nThis elevation is not driven by \\LaTeX{} syntax: stripping all markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$).\nCode (Python) provides an informative contrast: despite rigid syntactic structure, it yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, confirming that the arXiv elevation reflects academic conventions rather than structural complexity per se.\n$\\Kempirical$ varies by domain, suggesting that any future depth-aware filtering should account for source characteristics rather than assuming a universal boundary.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments across three families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (3v4 $\\auc = 0.68$--$0.80$); extending Pythia and Qwen to depth~7 sustains $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (6v7 $\\auc = 0.634$--$0.641 > \\theta$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings.\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}\nGreedy decoding inflates depth separability: Pythia-6.9B yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ under greedy but $\\Kempirical{=}1$ under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}).\n\n",
```

```json
    "newString": "Feature rankings are scorer-independent ($\\rho > 0.90$), but self-scoring inflates boundary AUC (OLMo: $+$11.5\\,pp, Pythia: $+$4.7\\,pp); cross-scoring provides a conservative estimate for deployment against unknown generators (length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nOne-wash laundering refers to this concentrated signal loss at the 0v1 boundary: while depth-1 remains marginally distinguishable from depth-2 (1v2 $\\auc$ 0.61--0.70, the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ finding), the dominant distributional shift occurs at generation $0{\\to}1$, and beyond depth~2 no further signal is exploitable.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nOne-wash laundering is distinct from model collapse~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse}: model collapse describes cumulative quality degradation across generations, while one-wash laundering describes the erasure of statistical fingerprints distinguishing adjacent generations; text quality may continue degrading even after the features separating generation $k$ from $k{+}1$ become indistinguishable.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nThe 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.\nThe gap between $\\Kempirical$ and $\\Ktheory$ remains open; a DeBERTa-v3-base upper-bound probe yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = \\Kempirical^{\\text{OBD}} = 2$ on C4 and Code but $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = 4$ on arXiv, placing the cost of generator-agnostic features at one depth level for structured text and zero for web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd}).\nFeature rankings are scorer-independent ($\\rho > 0.90$); $\\Kempirical$ agreement is scorer-independent for strong-signal configurations (e.g., Qwen-rewrite retains $\\Kempirical{=}2$ under all scorers) but scorer-sensitive at marginal boundaries (Qwen-continuation drops to $\\Kempirical{=}1$; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nSelf-scoring inflates pairwise AUC: OLMo shows the largest gap (1v2 $\\Delta$ = +11.5\\,pp; self-scored $\\Kempirical$=2 vs.\\ cross-scored $\\Kempirical$=1), followed by Pythia (+4.7\\,pp) and Mistral-7B (+2.4\\,pp).\nCross-scoring provides a conservative, deployment-realistic estimate of $K^*$, as real-world contamination originates from unknown generators.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) and Code (Python) consistently yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across three independent architecture families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), as formulaic structure (citations, math notation, section conventions) creates larger per-generation distributional shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4).\nThis elevation is not driven by \\LaTeX{} syntax: stripping all markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$); Code (Python), despite rigid syntax, yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, confirming that the elevation reflects academic conventions rather than structural complexity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments across three families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (3v4 $\\auc = 0.68$--$0.80$); extending Pythia and Qwen to depth~7 sustains $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (6v7 $\\auc = 0.634$--$0.641 > \\theta$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings.\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}\nGreedy decoding inflates depth separability: Pythia-6.9B yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ under greedy but $\\Kempirical{=}1$ under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}).\n\n",
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   This is a Paper Agent session for "gen-depth-contamination" targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR (DDL 2026-05-25). The session handled three sequential Director patch instructions:
   
   - **Round 29 page overflow fix** (completed earlier, continued here): Verifying and fixing the 8-page main text limit after Round 29 reviewer changes. Conclusion had overflowed to page 9.
   
   - **Fill 4-5 lines on page 8** (completed): Director noted page 8 wasn't full and requested expansions prioritized as: (1) Section 5 dose-response discussion, (2) Section 4.1 θ sensitivity, (3) Discussion Table 28 sample stability.
   
   - **Integrate three evidence data items** (in progress): Director provided bootstrap K* data, runtime benchmark data, and asked to continue the fill task. Three items: (1) Bootstrap K* stability table + main text summary (MF-3, highest priority), (2) Runtime benchmark table + optional throughput mention (SF-6), (3) Continue 4-5 line fill.

   The Paper Agent communicates exclusively via `paper_agent_post_to_director` (NOT `main_agent_post_to_director`). EMNLP 2026 enforces 8-page main text limit; Limitations/Ethics/References don't count.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - K*_empirical: discrimination boundary = max depth where AUC > θ=0.60
   - Bootstrap K* stability: 8/10 configurations show 100% consistency across 1,000 resamples
   - Within-sample (bootstrap) vs between-sample (5K-vs-10K) uncertainty bounding
   - Runtime: ~22 docs/sec (44.7 ms/doc total: 20.6ms scorer + 23.4ms features + 0.74ms LightGBM)
   - One-wash laundering: concentrated signal loss at 0v1 boundary
   - Generator-agnostic features: 15 statistical features, scoped to continuation-mode generators
   - OBD (Ordinal Binary Decomposition): LightGBM classifier
   - Page budget management: iterative compile-check-trim cycles in two-column LaTeX format

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`docs/paper/discussion.tex`** (MODIFIED heavily across all three patches)
     - Successive compressions to fit page 8 while adding new content elsewhere
     - Removed: "Boundary cases and scale effect" paragraph, "What K* reveals" paragraph, ANOVA sentence
     - Compressed: DeBERTa (2→1 sentence), Decoding confound (removed fold details), Scorer sentences (3→1), Domain dependence Code contrast (merged with LaTeX syntax sentence)
     - Current state (all paragraphs):
     ```latex
     \paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
     ```

```json
        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nOne-wash laundering refers to this concentrated signal loss at the 0v1 boundary: while depth-1 remains marginally distinguishable from depth-2 (1v2 $\\auc$ 0.61--0.70, the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ finding), the dominant distributional shift occurs at generation $0{\\to}1$, and beyond depth~2 no further signal is exploitable.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nOne-wash laundering is distinct from model collapse~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse}: model collapse describes cumulative quality degradation across generations, while one-wash laundering describes the erasure of statistical fingerprints distinguishing adjacent generations; text quality may continue degrading even after the features separating generation $k$ from $k{+}1$ become indistinguishable.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nThe 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.\nThe gap between $\\Kempirical$ and $\\Ktheory$ remains open; a DeBERTa-v3-base upper-bound probe yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = \\Kempirical^{\\text{OBD}} = 2$ on C4 and Code but $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = 4$ on arXiv, placing the cost of generator-agnostic features at one depth level for structured text and zero for web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd}).\nFeature rankings are scorer-independent ($\\rho > 0.90$), but self-scoring inflates boundary AUC (OLMo: $+$11.5\\,pp, Pythia: $+$4.7\\,pp); cross-scoring provides a conservative estimate for deployment against unknown generators (length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) and Code (Python) consistently yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across three independent architecture families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), as formulaic structure (citations, math notation, section conventions) creates larger per-generation distributional shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4).\nThis elevation is not driven by \\LaTeX{} syntax: stripping all markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$); Code (Python), despite rigid syntax, yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, confirming that the elevation reflects academic conventions rather than structural complexity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments across three families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (3v4 $\\auc = 0.68$--$0.80$); extending Pythia and Qwen to depth~7 sustains $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (6v7 $\\auc = 0.634$--$0.641 > \\theta$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings.\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}\nGreedy decoding inflates depth separability: Pythia-6.9B yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ under greedy but $\\Kempirical{=}1$ under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}).\n\n",
```

```json
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/method.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Corpus-level statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction. (Simplified notation; see Eq.~\\ref{eq:kstar} for the formal $\\Kempirical$ definition.)}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: mutual information with the seed text degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this motivates the existence of a finite discrimination boundary where $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ falls below a decision threshold, though AUC and mutual information are not formally equivalent; the actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.\nPairwise AUC may exhibit non-monotonicity at deeper depths due to classifier and feature interactions (e.g., Pythia-1.4B greedy 4v5 $\\auc = 1.0$ vs.\\ 3v4 $= 0.522$; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}), reinforcing that $\\Kempirical$ is a measured quantity rather than a theoretical prediction.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\!\\bigl\\{k \\!\\geq\\! 1 : \\auc_{j-1,j} > \\theta,\\;\\forall\\, 1 \\!\\leq\\! j \\!\\leq\\! k\\bigr\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich requires all pairs up to depth $k$ to exceed the threshold (the $\\forall j$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ decreases monotonically above $\\theta$ across all tested configurations; Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).\nThe theoretical upper bound $\\Ktheory$ (the true maximum discriminable depth given optimal features) is not directly estimated in this work.\nSince any specific classifier on a feature subset achieves AUC at most equal to the Bayes-optimal classifier on all features, $\\Kempirical \\leq \\Ktheory$ follows directly.\nThe DPI motivates the empirical observation that $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ generally decreases with $k$ under non-degenerate decoding, though AUC is not a monotone function of MI (as noted above).\nHere $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point; $\\theta$ sits between the 1v2 AUC cluster ($0.61$--$0.70$) and the 2v3 cluster ($0.55$--$0.59$), so that $\\Kempirical$ is insensitive to the exact threshold within this range (justified below; Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).\nAll pairwise significance tests use 10{,}000-iteration permutation tests with Bonferroni correction for 10 comparisons ($\\alpha_{\\text{corrected}}{=}0.005$); we report bootstrap 95\\% CIs (1{,}000 resamples unless otherwise noted) for all boundary-pair AUCs and explicitly mark marginal cases where Bonferroni-corrected lower bounds fall below $\\theta$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold justification.}\n$\\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by two independent lines of evidence.\nFirst, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).\nSecond, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity that ensures any $\\theta \\in [0.59, 0.61]$ yields the same $\\Kempirical$; only at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\\theta \\in [0.55, 0.70]$.\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).\nSelf-BLEU reference sets are drawn exclusively within each fold to prevent information leakage across cross-validation splits; self-BLEU ranks \\#1 in 1/8 conditions (greedy decoding) but is absent from the top-3 in all 7 others.\nRemoving self-BLEU reduces mean $\\auc_{1v2}$ by $< 1$\\,pp across all models (14D $\\Kempirical = 2$ for all 4 configurations); in deployment where reference corpora are unavailable, self-BLEU can be replaced with corpus-level self-similarity or omitted entirely, leaving 14 features (surprisal statistics, TTR, hapax ratio, frequency tail) that require no external reference and retain full $\\Kempirical$.\nThese features are generator-agnostic in that they require no knowledge of or access to the generating model, though they depend on an external scorer for surprisal computation (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).\nOBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate six primary configurations spanning three model families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base), Qwen2.5-7B, Pythia-1.4B, and OLMo-1B; four additional models are introduced in Section~\\ref{sec:experiments}.\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \\textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \\textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \\textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",
        "numLines": 78,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 78
      }
```

```json
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/conclusion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nAcross 10 prompt-chain configurations spanning six architecture families (1B--8B), $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ for all web text sources ($\\theta{=}0.60$); arXiv reaches $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across three families.\nThis one-wash laundering effect concentrates all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary; under extreme (100\\%) contamination, binary curation minimizes MMLU degradation ($-$0.77\\,pp vs.\\ $-$1.53\\,pp), though all strategies converge at realistic ratios.\nGenerator-agnostic features (within continuation mode) enable deployment without retraining (80--92\\% retention); extending $\\Kempirical$ to 13B+ scale remains open.\n",
        "numLines": 7,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 7
      }
    },
```

```json
    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",
```
